assertEq(seized, previewSeized);\n        assertLt(repaid, debtBefore); // collateral cannot cover the whole debt\n        assertEq(seized, 10e18); // all of it is taken in one close-out\n        assertEq(market.collateralOf(alice), 0);\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(bob), seized);\n        // The liquidator's take is worth at least what they paid.\n        assertGe(seized * newPrice / 1e20, repaid);\n    }","originalFile":"// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT\npragma solidity 0.8.28;\n\nimport {Test} from \"forge-std/Test.sol\";\n\nimport {ChainlinkPriceOracle} from \"../src/ChainlinkPriceOracle.sol\";\nimport {LendingMarket} from \"../src/LendingMarket.sol\";\nimport {MockAggregator} from \"./mocks/MockAggregator.sol\";\nimport {MockERC20} from \"./mocks/MockERC20.sol\";\n\ncontract LendingMarketTest is Test {\n    MockERC20 weth;\n    MockERC20 usdc;\n    MockAggregator feed;\n    ChainlinkPriceOracle oracle;\n    LendingMarket market;\n\n    address owner = address(0xA11CE);\n    address alice = address(0xBEEF);\n    address bob = address(0xCAFE);\n\n    uint256 constant START_PRICE = 2_000e8; // $2000 / ETH, 8 decimals\n\n    function setUp() public {\n        vm.warp(1_700_000_000);\n\n        weth = new MockERC20(\"Wrapped Ether\", \"WETH\", 18);\n        usdc = new MockERC20(\"USD Coin\", \"USDC\", 6);\n        feed = new MockAggregator(8, int256(START_PRICE));\n        oracle = new ChainlinkPriceOracle(address(feed), 3900, 100e8, 100_000e8);\n\n        market = new LendingMarket(\n            LendingMarket.Params({\n                owner: owner,\n                collateralToken: address(weth),\n                debtToken: address(usdc),\n                oracle: address(oracle),\n                maxLtvBps: 7_000,\n                liquidationThresholdBps: 8_500,\n                liquidationBonusBps: 500,\n                closeFactorBps: 5_000,\n                borrowRateBps: 500,\n                borrowCap: 2_000_000e6,\n                collateralCap: 1_000e18,\n                minDebt: 500e6\n            })\n        );\n\n        usdc.mint(owner, 1_000_000e6);\n        vm.startPrank(owner);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        market.supply(1_000_000e6);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        weth.mint(alice, 100e18);\n        usdc.mint(bob, 1_000_000e6);\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------ helpers ------------------------------ */\n\n    function _deposit(address who, uint256 amount) internal {\n        vm.startPrank(who);\n        weth.approve(address(market), amount);\n        market.depositCollateral(amount);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    function _borrow(address who, uint256 amount) internal {\n        vm.prank(who);\n        market.borrow(amount, who);\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------- scaling ----------------------------- */\n\n    function test_priceScale_derivedFromDecimals() public view {\n        // 18 (WETH) + 8 (feed) - 6 (USDC)\n        assertEq(market.priceScale(), 1e20);\n    }\n\n    function test_collateralValue_usesSixDecimals() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 1e18);\n        // 1 WETH @ $2000 => 2000 USDC (6 decimals)\n        assertEq(market.collateralValueOf(alice), 2_000e6);\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------ borrowing ---------------------------- */\n\n    function test_borrowUpTo70Percent() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18); // $20,000\n        assertEq(market.maxBorrowable(alice), 14_000e6);\n\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n        assertEq(usdc.balanceOf(alice), 14_000e6);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(alice), 14_000e6);\n    }\n\n    function test_borrowAbove70PercentReverts() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        vm.prank(alice);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.borrow(14_000e6 + 1, alice);\n    }\n\n    function test_borrowBelowMinDebtReverts() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        vm.prank(alice);\n        vm.expectRevert(abi.encodeWithSelector(LendingMarket.DebtBelowMinimum.selector, 499e6, 500e6));\n        market.borrow(499e6, alice);\n    }\n\n    function test_borrowRespectsCap() public {\n        vm.prank(owner);\n        market.setBorrowCap(1_000e6);\n\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        vm.prank(alice);\n        vm.expectRevert(abi.encodeWithSelector(LendingMarket.BorrowCapExceeded.selector, 1_001e6, 1_000e6));\n        market.borrow(1_001e6, alice);\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------- interest ---------------------------- */\n\n    function test_interestAccruesAtFlatRate() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 10_000e6);\n\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 365 days);\n        // 5% flat on 10,000 USDC == 500 USDC (rounding up by at most 1 unit)\n        assertApproxEqAbs(market.debtOf(alice), 10_500e6, 1);\n    }\n\n    function test_noRetroactiveInterestBeforeFirstBorrow() public {\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 365 days);\n        feed.setAnswer(int256(START_PRICE)); // refresh the feed after the warp\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 10_000e6);\n        assertApproxEqAbs(market.debtOf(alice), 10_000e6, 1);\n    }\n\n    /* -------------------------- repay / withdraw ------------------------- */\n\n    function test_repayFullBurnsAllShares() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 10_000e6);\n\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 30 days);\n        uint256 debt = market.debtOf(alice);\n\n        usdc.mint(alice, debt);\n        vm.startPrank(alice);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        market.repay(type(uint256).max, alice);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(alice), 0);\n        assertEq(market.debtSharesOf(alice), 0);\n        assertEq(market.totalDebtShares(), 0);\n        assertGe(usdc.balanceOf(address(market)), 1_000_000e6 - 10_000e6 + debt);\n    }\n\n    function test_repayWorksWhileOracleIsDown() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 10_000e6);\n\n        // Feed goes stale — borrow/withdraw must fail, repay must still work.\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 10 days);\n\n        vm.prank(alice);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.withdrawCollateral(1e18, alice);\n\n        usdc.mint(alice, 1_000e6);\n        vm.startPrank(alice);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        market.repay(type(uint256).max, alice);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(alice), 0);\n    }\n\n    function test_withdrawBlockedIfItBreachesLtv() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n\n        vm.prank(alice);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.withdrawCollateral(1, alice);\n    }\n\n    function test_withdrawAllWhenNoDebt() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        vm.prank(alice);\n        market.withdrawCollateral(10e18, alice);\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(alice), 100e18);\n        assertEq(market.totalCollateral(), 0);\n    }\n\n    function test_pauseDoesNotTrapUsers() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 10_000e6);\n\n        vm.prank(owner);\n        market.pause();\n\n        // New risk blocked...\n        vm.startPrank(alice);\n        weth.approve(address(market), 1e18);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.depositCollateral(1e18);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.borrow(1e6, alice);\n\n        // ...but exits stay open.\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        market.repay(type(uint256).max, alice);\n        market.withdrawCollateral(10e18, alice);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    /* ------------------------------ liquidation -------------------------- */\n\n    function test_healthyPositionCannotBeLiquidated() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.liquidate(alice, 1_000e6, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    function test_liquidationPaysFivePercentBonus() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6); // 70% of $20,000\n\n        // ETH to $1600 => collateral $16,000, threshold value $13,600 < debt $14,000\n        feed.setAnswer(1_600e8);\n        assertLt(market.healthFactor(alice), 1e18);\n\n        (uint256 previewRepaid, uint256 previewSeized) = market.previewLiquidation(alice, 7_000e6);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(alice, 7_000e6, previewSeized, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(repaid, previewRepaid);\n        assertEq(seized, previewSeized);\n        assertEq(repaid, 7_000e6); // close factor = 50% of 14,000\n        // 7000 USDC * 1.05 / 1600 = 4.59375 WETH\n        assertEq(seized, 4.59375e18);\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(bob), seized);\n        assertGe(market.healthFactor(alice), 1e18); // restored to healthy\n    }\n\n    function test_closeFactorCapsRepayment() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n        feed.setAnswer(1_600e8);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        (uint256 repaid,) = market.liquidate(alice, type(uint256).max, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(repaid, 7_000e6);\n    }\n\n    function test_underwaterPositionAllowsFullLiquidation() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n\n        // ETH to $1300 => collateral $13,000 < debt $14,000\n        feed.setAnswer(1_300e8);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(alice, type(uint256).max, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        // Collateral runs out before the full debt can be repaid at a 5% bonus.\n        assertEq(seized, 10e18);\n        assertEq(repaid, uint256(13_000e6) * 10_000 / 10_500);\n        assertEq(market.collateralOf(alice), 0);\n        assertGt(market.debtOf(alice), 0); // residual bad debt\n    }\n\n    function test_slippageGuardRevertsOnPriceMove() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n        feed.setAnswer(1_600e8);\n\n        (, uint256 expected) = market.previewLiquidation(alice, 7_000e6);\n\n        // Price recovers before inclusion; the same repayment now buys less WETH.\n        feed.setAnswer(1_610e8);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.liquidate(alice, 7_000e6, expected, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    function test_liquidationNeverLeavesDustDebt() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n        feed.setAnswer(1_600e8);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        (uint256 repaid,) = market.liquidate(alice, type(uint256).max, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(repaid, 7_000e6);\n        uint256 remaining = market.debtOf(alice);\n        assertTrue(remaining == 0 || remaining >= market.minDebt());\n    }\n\n    function test_repayAmountIsAHardCeiling() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n        // Cv = $14,500 < debt * 1.05, so the whole position must be closed at once.\n        feed.setAnswer(1_450e8);\n\n        (uint256 required,) = market.previewLiquidation(alice, 1_000e6);\n        assertEq(required, 14_000e6);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        // Asking to repay only $1,000 must revert rather than pull the full $14,000.\n        vm.expectRevert(abi.encodeWithSelector(LendingMarket.FullCloseRequired.selector, 14_000e6));\n        market.liquidate(alice, 1_000e6, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(usdc.balanceOf(bob), 1_000_000e6); // nothing pulled\n    }\n\n    function test_writeOffBadDebt() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n        feed.setAnswer(1_300e8);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        market.liquidate(alice, type(uint256).max, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertGt(market.debtOf(alice), 0);\n        vm.prank(owner);\n        market.writeOffBadDebt(alice);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(alice), 0);\n        assertEq(market.totalDebtShares(), 0);\n    }\n\n    /* -------------------------------- oracle ----------------------------- */\n\n    function test_stalePriceReverts() public {\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 4000);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        oracle.price();\n    }\n\n    function test_nonPositiveAnswerReverts() public {\n        feed.setAnswer(0);\n        vm.expectRevert(ChainlinkPriceOracle.NonPositiveAnswer.selector);\n        oracle.price();\n    }\n\n    function test_outOfBandAnswerReverts() public {\n        feed.setAnswer(50e8); // below the $100 floor\n        vm.expectRevert(abi.encodeWithSelector(ChainlinkPriceOracle.PriceOutOfBounds.selector, 50e8));\n        oracle.price();\n    }\n\n    function test_incompleteRoundReverts() public {\n        feed.setAnsweredInRound(0);\n        vm.expectRevert(ChainlinkPriceOracle.StaleRound.selector);\n        oracle.price();\n    }\n\n    /* ----------------------------- access control ------------------------ */\n\n    function test_onlyOwnerCanTouchParameters() public {\n        vm.startPrank(alice);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.setBorrowRateBps(100);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.pause();\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.withdrawSupply(1e6, alice);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.writeOffBadDebt(bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    function test_rateIsCapped() public {\n        vm.prank(owner);\n        vm.expectRevert(LendingMarket.InvalidParameters.selector);\n        market.setBorrowRateBps(5_001);\n    }\n\n    function test_ownerCannotSweepUserAssets() public {\n        vm.startPrank(owner);\n        vm.expectRevert(LendingMarket.InvalidParameters.selector);\n        market.sweep(address(weth), owner, 1);\n        vm.expectRevert(LendingMarket.InvalidParameters.selector);\n        market.sweep(address(usdc), owner, 1);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    function test_ownerCannotWithdrawCollateralViaSupplyWithdrawal() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        // withdrawSupply is bounded by the USDC balance and never touches WETH.\n        vm.prank(owner);\n        market.withdrawSupply(1_000_000e6, owner);\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(address(market)), 10e18);\n        assertEq(market.collateralOf(alice), 10e18);\n    }\n\n    /* -------------------------------- fuzz ------------------------------- */\n\n    /// @dev Solvency invariant: a borrower can never walk away with more debt-token value\n    ///      than their collateral was worth at the max LTV.\n    function testFuzz_cannotBorrowBeyondLtv(uint256 collateral, uint256 borrowAmount) public {\n        collateral = bound(collateral, 1e15, 100e18);\n        uint256 limit = collateral * START_PRICE / 1e20 * 7_000 / 10_000;\n        vm.assume(limit >= 500e6);\n        borrowAmount = bound(borrowAmount, 500e6, limit + 1e6);\n\n        _deposit(alice, collateral);\n        vm.prank(alice);\n        if (borrowAmount > limit) {\n            vm.expectRevert();\n            market.borrow(borrowAmount, alice);\n        } else {\n            market.borrow(borrowAmount, alice);\n            assertGe(market.healthFactor(alice), 1e18);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// @dev While a position is still solvent (collateral worth at least the debt), a\n    ///      liquidation must never make it worse: either health improves, or the position is\n    ///      closed out entirely. This is the property the full-close band exists to preserve.\n    function testFuzz_solventLiquidationNeverDegradesPosition(uint256 newPrice, uint256 repayAmount) public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n\n        // Cv >= $14,000 == debt, so the position is unhealthy but not yet insolvent.\n        newPrice = bound(newPrice, 1_400e8, 1_640e8);\n        feed.setAnswer(int256(newPrice));\n        vm.assume(market.healthFactor(alice) < 1e18);\n\n        repayAmount = bound(repayAmount, 1e6, 14_000e6);\n        (uint256 previewRepaid,) = market.previewLiquidation(alice, repayAmount);\n        vm.assume(previewRepaid > 0);\n        // Skip the cases the forced close-out band rejects outright; they pull nothing.\n        vm.assume(previewRepaid <= repayAmount);\n\n        uint256 hfBefore = market.healthFactor(alice);\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        market.liquidate(alice, repayAmount, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertTrue(market.healthFactor(alice) >= hfBefore || market.collateralOf(alice) == 0);\n        // A solvent position must never be turned into bad debt.\n        assertTrue(market.debtOf(alice) == 0 || market.collateralOf(alice) > 0);\n    }\n\n    /// @dev Once a position is genuinely insolvent, paying any bonus at all necessarily grows\n    ///      the shortfall — no liquidation rule avoids that. What must still hold is that\n    ///      every liquidation makes strict progress and that `previewLiquidation` is exact.\n    function testFuzz_insolventLiquidationMakesProgress(uint256 newPrice, uint256 repayAmount) public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n\n        // Cv < $14,000 == debt: underwater.\n        newPrice = bound(newPrice, 900e8, 1_399e8);\n        feed.setAnswer(int256(newPrice));\n\n        repayAmount = bound(repayAmount, 1e6, 14_000e6);\n        (uint256 previewRepaid, uint256 previewSeized) = market.previewLiquidation(alice, repayAmount);\n        vm.assume(previewRepaid > 0);\n\n        uint256 debtBefore = market.debtOf(alice);\n        uint256 collateralBefore = market.collateralOf(alice);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(alice, repayAmount, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(repaid, previewRepaid);\n        assertEq(seized, previewSeized);\n        assertLt(market.debtOf(alice), debtBefore);\n        assertLt(market.collateralOf(alice), collateralBefore);\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(bob), seized);\n    }\n\n    /// @dev Repeated liquidations must converge: a position can never be ground down\n    ///      forever while staying liquidatable.\n    function test_liquidationConvergesInTheThinBand() public {\n        _deposit(alice, 10e18);\n        _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);\n\n        // Cv = $14,500, debt = $14,000. Cv < debt * 1.05, so a capped 50% liquidation\n        // would make health *worse*; the full-close rule must kick in instead.\n        feed.setAnswer(1_450e8);\n        assertLt(market.healthFactor(alice), 1e18);\n\n        vm.startPrank(bob);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        (uint256 repaid,) = market.liquidate(alice, type(uint256).max, 0, bob);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(repaid, 14_000e6); // whole debt, not the 50% close factor\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(alice), 0); // no bad debt manufactured\n        // All 10 WETH ($14,500) goes to the liquidator for a $14,000 repayment: a 3.6%\n        // bonus rather than the headline 5%, because that is all the collateral supports.\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(bob), 10e18);\n        assertEq(market.collateralOf(alice), 0);\n    }\n}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":471,"oldLines":7,"newStart":471,"newLines":7,"lines":["     /// @dev Once a position is genuinely insolvent, paying any bonus at all necessarily grows","     ///      the shortfall — no liquidation rule avoids that. What must still hold is that","     ///      every liquidation makes strict progress and that `previewLiquidation` is exact.","-    function testFuzz_insolventLiquidationMakesProgress(uint256 newPrice, uint256 repayAmount) public {","+    function testFuzz_insolventLiquidationMakesProgress(uint256 newPrice) public {","         _deposit(alice, 10e18);","         _borrow(alice, 14_000e6);"," "]},{"oldStart":479,"oldLines":23,"newStart":479,"newLines":24,"lines":["         newPrice = bound(newPrice, 900e8, 1_399e8);","         feed.setAnswer(int256(newPrice));"," ","-        repayAmount = bound(repayAmount, 1e6, 14_000e6);","-        (uint256 previewRepaid, uint256 previewSeized) = market.previewLiquidation(alice, repayAmount);","+        (uint256 previewRepaid, uint256 previewSeized) = market.previewLiquidation(alice, type(uint256).max);","         vm.assume(previewRepaid > 0);"," ","         uint256 debtBefore = market.debtOf(alice);","-        uint256 collateralBefore = market.collateralOf(alice);"," ","         vm.startPrank(bob);","         usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);","-        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(alice, repayAmount, 0, bob);","+        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(alice, type(uint256).max, 0, bob);","         vm.stopPrank();"," ","         assertEq(repaid, previewRepaid);","         assertEq(seized, previewSeized);","-        assertLt(market.debtOf(alice), debtBefore);","-        assertLt(market.collateralOf(alice), collateralBefore);","+        assertLt(repaid, debtBefore); // collateral cannot cover the whole debt","+        assertEq(seized, 10e18); // all of it is taken in one close-out","+        assertEq(market.collateralOf(alice), 0);","         assertEq(weth.balanceOf(bob), seized);","+        // The liquidator's take is worth at least what they paid.","+        assertGe(seized * newPrice / 1e20, repaid);","     }"," ","     /// @dev Repeated liquidations must converge: a position can never be ground down"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TTjCJpZQ1GoW4yc2YA4NGw","type":"tool_result","content":"    ///        Set this. A price move between broadcast and inclusion changes the payout.\n    /// @param to Recipient of the seized collateral.\n    /// @return debtRepaid Debt-token amount actually pulled from the caller.\n    /// @return collateralSeized Collateral-token amount actually sent to `to`.\n    function liquidate(address account, uint256 repayAmount, uint256 minCollateralOut, address to)\n        external\n        nonReentrant\n        returns (uint256 debtRepaid, uint256 collateralSeized)\n    {\n        if (account == address(0) || to == address(0)) revert ZeroAddress();\n        if (repayAmount == 0) revert ZeroAmount();\n\n        accrueInterest();\n\n        (debtRepaid, collateralSeized) = _quoteLiquidation(account, repayAmount, borrowIndex, true);\n\n        if (debtRepaid == 0 || collateralSeized == 0) revert NothingToLiquidate();\n        // `repayAmount` is a hard ceiling on what we pull, not just a target: the dust rule and\n        // the forced close-out band can both push the required repayment above what was asked.\n        if (debtRepaid > repayAmount) revert FullCloseRequired(debtRepaid);\n        if (collateralSeized < minCollateralOut) revert SlippageExceeded(collateralSeized, minCollateralOut);\n\n        uint256 sharesBurned = _applyLiquidation(account, debtRepaid, collateralSeized);\n\n        // ---- interactions ----\n        if (_pullToken(debtToken, msg.sender, debtRepaid) != debtRepaid) revert UnexpectedTokenBehaviour();\n        collateralToken.safeTransfer(to, collateralSeized);\n\n        emit Liquidated(msg.sender, account, debtRepaid, collateralSeized, sharesBurned);\n    }\n\n    /// @dev State updates for a liquidation. Split out of `liquidate` to keep the stack shallow.\n    /// @return sharesBurned Debt shares removed from `account`.\n    function _applyLiquidation(address account, uint256 debtRepaid, uint256 collateralSeized)\n        internal\n        returns (uint256 sharesBurned)\n    {\n        uint256 shares = debtSharesOf[account];\n\n        // Round burned shares up so rounding never costs the protocol; cap at the balance.\n        sharesBurned = Math.mulDiv(debtRepaid, RAY, borrowIndex, Math.Rounding.Ceil);\n        if (sharesBurned > shares) sharesBurned = shares;\n\n        uint256 remainingShares = shares - sharesBurned;\n        uint256 remainingCollateral = collateralOf[account] - collateralSeized;\n\n        debtSharesOf[account] = remainingShares;\n        totalDebtShares -= sharesBurned;\n        collateralOf[account] = remainingCollateral;\n        totalCollateral -= collateralSeized;\n\n        if (remainingCollateral == 0 && remainingShares != 0) {\n            emit BadDebtRecorded(account, _sharesToDebt(remainingShares), remainingShares);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// @dev Shared liquidation math for `liquidate` and `previewLiquidation`.\n    /// @param revertIfHealthy When true, revert on a healthy position; when false, return zeros.\n    function _quoteLiquidation(address account, uint256 repayAmount, uint256 index, bool revertIfHealthy)\n        internal\n        view\n        returns (uint256 debtRepaid, uint256 collateralSeized)\n    {\n        uint256 shares = debtSharesOf[account];\n        if (shares == 0) {\n            if (revertIfHealthy) revert NoDebt();\n            return (0, 0);\n        }\n\n        uint256 collateral = collateralOf[account];\n        uint256 debt = _sharesToDebt(shares, index);\n        uint256 price = oracle.price();\n        uint256 collateralValue = _collateralValue(collateral, price);\n\n        // Health is checked at the liquidation threshold, not the max LTV.\n        uint256 hf = _healthFactor(collateralValue, debt);\n        if (hf >= HEALTH_FACTOR_SCALE) {\n            if (revertIfHealthy) revert PositionHealthy(hf);\n            return (0, 0);\n        }\n\n        // Close factor: a partial bite while the collateral still covers debt-plus-bonus,\n        // an all-or-nothing close-out below that.\n        //\n        // The cutoff is not arbitrary. A partial liquidation moves the health factor from\n        // Cv/D to (Cv - r(1+b))/(D - r), which is an improvement only while Cv > D * (1 + b).\n        // At or below that point every partial bite drives the position *further* underwater\n        // while leaving it liquidatable: repeated liquidations grind a solvent position down\n        // into bad debt instead of restoring it. So in that band the whole position must be\n        // closed at once — merely *permitting* a full close is not enough, since a liquidator\n        // would still be free to take a small, position-degrading bite.\n        uint256 fullCloseAt = Math.mulDiv(debt, BPS + liquidationBonusBps, BPS);\n        if (collateralValue <= fullCloseAt) {\n            debtRepaid = debt;\n        } else {\n            uint256 maxRepay = Math.mulDiv(debt, closeFactorBps, BPS);\n            debtRepaid = repayAmount > maxRepay ? maxRepay : repayAmount;\n\n            // Never leave a position with dust debt that nobody would pay gas to clear.\n            if (debt - debtRepaid < minDebt) debtRepaid = debt;\n        }\n\n        // Collateral owed = repaid value grossed up by the bonus, converted at the oracle price.\n        // Round down: the borrower keeps rounding dust, not the liquidator.\n        collateralSeized = _debtValueToCollateral(Math.mulDiv(debtRepaid, BPS + liquidationBonusBps, BPS), price);\n\n        if (collateralSeized > collateral) {","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d85eed9d-2882-43d6-9ace-40e073b33c51","uuid":"4b7cce1c-2de3-4a6d-828a-2df64e38b2d0","timestamp":"2026-07-28T12:41:29.292Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"    ///        Set this. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdUUNdeuGHETxyM74MWfw","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01D7uwDWtFbyqwvUDTSUaY4s","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/security-goal-002/2026-07-28T122811Z-claude-with-skill-2/workspace/NOTES.md","content":"# WETH/USDC borrowing market — operator & integrator notes\n\nTwo contracts:\n\n| Contract | Role |\n|---|---|\n| `src/ChainlinkPriceOracle.sol` | Immutable wrapper around one Chainlink feed. No owner, no setters. |\n| `src/LendingMarket.sol` | The market. Holds collateral, tracks debt, handles liquidation. |\n\n`script/Deploy.s.sol` deploys both with mainnet addresses and the parameters below.\n\nParameters as deployed: **max LTV 70%**, **liquidation threshold 85%**, **liquidation bonus\n5%**, **close factor 50%**, **5% flat APR**.\n\n---\n\n## 1. How a position's health is computed\n\n### Units\n\nNothing assumes 18 decimals. The three scales in play are:\n\n| Quantity | Decimals |\n|---|---|\n| WETH collateral | 18 |\n| USDC debt | 6 |\n| Chainlink ETH/USD answer | 8 |\n\nThe market reads all three from the tokens and the feed at construction and derives a single\nconstant:\n\n```\npriceScale = 10 ** (collateralDecimals + priceDecimals - debtDecimals)   // 10^(18+8-6) = 1e20\n```\n\nEverything is then denominated in **USDC units (6 decimals)** as the unit of account:\n\n```\ncollateralValue = collateralAmount * price / priceScale\n```\n\n`10 WETH` at a feed answer of `2000e8` gives `10e18 * 2000e8 / 1e20 = 20_000e6` — $20,000\nexpressed in USDC's 6 decimals. All the math uses `Math.mulDiv`, which carries the full\n512-bit intermediate, so the multiply-before-divide never overflows and never truncates early.\n\n### Debt\n\nDebt is stored as index-normalised **shares**, not as a balance:\n\n```\nborrowIndex starts at 1e27 (RAY) and only grows\non borrow:  shares += amount * RAY / borrowIndex      (rounded UP)\ndebt owed  = shares * borrowIndex / RAY                (rounded UP)\n```\n\n`accrueInterest()` moves the index forward whenever anything touches the market:\n\n```\nborrowIndex += borrowIndex * borrowRateBps * elapsedSeconds / (10_000 * 365 days)\n```\n\nThat is simple interest over each elapsed window, applied on top of the running index — so it\ncompounds across accruals, at whatever cadence the market is used. At 5% APR the difference\nfrom strict simple interest is small but not zero; it is not a rounding artifact, it is the\nmodel. Every rounding decision goes **against the borrower and in favour of the protocol**\n(shares round up when borrowing, down when repaying, debt rounds up when read), so rounding\ndust can never accumulate into a shortfall.\n\nIf nobody borrows for a year and then someone does, they are not charged for that year: the\naccrual clock advances even when `totalDebtShares == 0`.\n\n### Health factor\n\n```\nhealthFactor = collateralValue * liquidationThresholdBps / 10_000 * 1e18 / debt\n```\n\n- `healthFactor >= 1e18` → healthy.\n- `healthFactor < 1e18` → liquidatable.\n- No debt → `type(uint256).max`.\n\n**Two different ratios are used, and the distinction matters:**\n\n| Ratio | Value | Where it applies |\n|---|---|---|\n| Max LTV | 70% | `borrow`, `withdrawCollateral` — the *entry* gate |\n| Liquidation threshold | 85% | `liquidate` — the *exit* gate |\n\nA borrower can only take an action that leaves them at or under 70%. They become liquidatable\nat 85%. The 15-point gap is the buffer that stops a position from being openable and instantly\nliquidatable.\n\nWorked example — 10 WETH deposited, ETH at $2,000:\n\n```\ncollateralValue    = $20,000\nmax borrow (70%)   = $14,000\nliquidation at 85% = debt > $17,000, or equivalently ETH < debt / (10 * 0.85)\n```\n\nBorrow the full $14,000 and the position goes underwater at `14000 / 8.5 = $1,647.06` per ETH —\nan 18% drop.\n\nViews for integrators: `healthFactor`, `debtOf`, `collateralValueOf`, `maxBorrowable`,\n`maxWithdrawable`. All of them apply pending interest first, so they agree with what a\ntransaction in the same block would see.\n\n---\n\n## 2. What a liquidator has to do\n\n### The call\n\n```solidity\nmarket.liquidate(\n    address account,           // the borrower\n    uint256 repayAmount,       // ceiling on USDC you will pay; type(uint256).max = largest allowed\n    uint256 minCollateralOut,  // slippage guard in WETH — SET THIS\n    address to                 // where the seized WETH goes\n) returns (uint256 debtRepaid, uint256 collateralSeized);\n```\n\nPrerequisites: hold the USDC and `approve` the market for at least `debtRepaid`. You receive\nWETH (not ETH — unwrap yourself if you want ether).\n\n### Steps\n\n1. Watch positions. Off-chain, track `Borrowed` / `Repaid` / `CollateralDeposited` /\n   `CollateralWithdrawn` / `Liquidated` events to maintain the borrower set, and poll\n   `healthFactor(account)`. Anything under `1e18` is liquidatable.\n2. Call `previewLiquidation(account, repayAmount)` — a pure view returning the exact\n   `(debtRepaid, collateralSeized)` the real call would produce. Size your USDC approval from\n   `debtRepaid`, and your `minCollateralOut` from `collateralSeized`.\n3. Send the transaction with a real `minCollateralOut`. Do not pass `0`.\n\n### What you get\n\n```\ncollateralSeized = debtRepaid * (10_000 + 500) / 10_000 * priceScale / price\n```\n\nRepay $7,000 of USDC with ETH at $1,600 and you receive `7000 * 1.05 / 1600 = 4.59375 WETH`\n— $7,350 of collateral for $7,000, a $350 gross profit before gas.\n\n### How much you are allowed to repay\n\nLet `Cv` be collateral value and `D` be the debt.\n\n- **`Cv > D * 1.05`** — normal case. You may repay up to the **50% close factor**. `repayAmount`\n  is clamped down to it; anything larger just takes the maximum bite.\n- **`Cv <= D * 1.05`** — you must close out the **entire** position in one call. Partial\n  liquidation is refused here, and this is the one piece of behaviour most likely to surprise\n  you.\n\nThe reason for the second rule: a partial liquidation moves health from `Cv/D` to\n`(Cv - r(1+b))/(D - r)`, which is an improvement **only while `Cv > D * (1+b)`**. Below that\npoint each partial bite drives the position further underwater while leaving it liquidatable —\na still-solvent position gets ground down into bad debt by repeated small liquidations. So the\nmarket forces the whole thing to be closed at once instead.\n\nTwo consequences for you:\n\n- If your `repayAmount` is below what the close-out requires, the call reverts with\n  `FullCloseRequired(required)` and pulls nothing. `repayAmount` is a hard ceiling, never a\n  target the contract exceeds. Always `previewLiquidation` first.\n- In the band `D <= Cv < D * 1.05` your bonus is **less than 5%** — you pay the full debt and\n  receive all the collateral, so you earn `Cv - D`, which is somewhere between 0% and 5%. The\n  alternative would be to scale your repayment down to protect your headline bonus, which\n  would manufacture bad debt out of a solvent position. The market will not do that.\n\nThere is also a **dust rule**: if a partial liquidation would leave the borrower with less than\n`minDebt` (500 USDC) outstanding, the repayment is rounded up to the full debt, so no position\nis ever left too small to be worth the gas to liquidate.\n\n### Bad debt\n\nIf the collateral cannot cover the debt even at a 0% bonus, you seize all of it, repay\n`Cv / 1.05`, and the remainder is a loss to the supply side. The market emits\n`BadDebtRecorded`. It does **not** socialise the loss across other borrowers; the owner clears\nthe accounting entry with `writeOffBadDebt(account)`.\n\n### MEV\n\nLiquidations are competitive and public. Assume every profitable one is contested:\n\n- `minCollateralOut` is your only on-chain protection against the price moving between\n  broadcast and inclusion. Set it from `previewLiquidation` with a tolerance you have chosen.\n- Send through a private mempool (Flashbots Protect, MEV-Blocker) — a public liquidation\n  transaction is trivially frontrun by a searcher who copies it.\n- Back-run the Chainlink feed update rather than polling on a timer: `ChainlinkPriceOracle`\n  reads `latestRoundData()`, so a position becomes liquidatable in the same block the feed\n  updates. That transaction is the signal.\n\n---\n\n## 3. What the operator has to get right on mainnet\n\n### Before the first deploy\n\n- [ ] **Verify the three addresses in `script/Deploy.s.sol` against Etherscan.** WETH\n      `0xC02aaA...756Cc2`, USDC `0xA0b869...06eB48`, ETH/USD feed `0x5f4eC3...5b8419`. A wrong\n      feed address is a silent total loss, not a revert.\n- [ ] **Point the oracle at the Chainlink *proxy*, not the underlying aggregator.** The\n      aggregator gets rotated; the proxy is the stable address.\n- [ ] **Set `maxStaleness` from the feed's real heartbeat.** Mainnet ETH/USD is 3600s, so the\n      script uses 3900s. Too tight and the market halts on a slow round; too loose and you\n      price positions off yesterday's number.\n- [ ] **Sanity-check `minPrice`/`maxPrice`.** The script uses $100–$100,000. These exist to\n      reject a broken or migrated feed. Note the failure mode they *cannot* fully fix: the\n      underlying Chainlink aggregator has its own `minAnswer`/`maxAnswer` circuit breakers, and\n      during a genuine crash below `minAnswer` the feed keeps reporting the floor. That is the\n      Venus/BNB failure mode. If ETH ever traded below your `minPrice`, this market would value\n      collateral too high and under-liquidate.\n- [ ] **Run `slither .` and `forge test --fuzz-runs 10000` and resolve everything.** Neither\n      slither nor mythril is installed in this workspace, so **the static-analysis pass has not\n      been run** — it is still outstanding and must happen before deploy. The test suite\n      (33 tests, fuzz included) does pass at 10,000 runs.\n- [ ] **Get an audit.** This is unaudited code holding user collateral.\n\n### Ownership\n\n- [ ] **`OWNER` must be a multisig or timelock, never an EOA.** The deploy script enforces\n      `owner.code.length > 0` as a blunt guard against fat-fingering an EOA, but that only\n      proves it is a contract, not that it is a *good* contract.\n- [ ] Ownership uses `Ownable2Step` — the new owner must accept. Don't leave a pending\n      transfer half-finished.\n- [ ] Put `setBorrowRateBps` behind a timelock. It is bounded at 50% APR in code, but a rate\n      change is still an economic action users deserve notice of.\n\n### What the owner can and cannot do\n\nWorth being explicit with users, because it defines the trust model:\n\n| Can | Cannot |\n|---|---|\n| Set the borrow rate (capped at 50% APR) | Change the oracle — it is `immutable` |\n| Set borrow / collateral caps and `minDebt` | Change LTV, threshold, bonus, or close factor — all `immutable` |\n| Pause new deposits and new borrows | Block `repay`, `withdrawCollateral`, or `liquidate` |\n| Withdraw idle USDC (`withdrawSupply`) | Touch WETH collateral — `sweep` rejects both market tokens |\n| Write off bad debt on a zero-collateral position | Seize or move a healthy borrower's position |\n\nTwo deliberate choices behind that table:\n\n**Pause is not a freeze.** `pause()` gates `depositCollateral` and `borrow` only. Repay,\nwithdraw, and liquidate stay open. A pause that traps a healthy borrower's collateral is a\ncensorship vector and a liquidation-cascade risk, so this one cannot. Even so, a single key\nthat can halt new borrowing is real power — it belongs behind the same multisig, and users\nshould be told it exists.\n\n**The oracle cannot be swapped.** Migrating to a new feed means deploying a new market and\nletting users migrate. That is inconvenient by design: an oracle address that a hot key can\nchange is a total-loss switch.\n\n### Liquidity\n\nThis market has **no public supplier side**. The owner supplies USDC with `supply()` and\nwithdraws it with `withdrawSupply()`; interest accrues to that same pool. This keeps the\ncontract small and sidesteps lender-share accounting and the ERC-4626 inflation attack\nentirely — but it means:\n\n- The owner can withdraw all idle USDC at any time. That cannot touch collateral or claw back\n  an outstanding loan, but it can leave the market unable to serve new borrows. It is a real\n  trust assumption; state it publicly.\n- Adding public depositors later is **not** a parameter change. It needs a share token with a\n  virtual-offset mitigation, a reserve factor, and a utilisation-aware rate model. That is a\n  different contract, not a config flag.\n\n### Caps and sizing\n\n- [ ] Start `borrowCap` and `collateralCap` small (the script defaults to 2M USDC / 1000 WETH)\n      and raise them as the market proves out. Lowering a cap only blocks new borrows; it never\n      makes an existing position liquidatable.\n- [ ] `minDebt` (500 USDC) must stay above the gas cost of a liquidation. If mainnet gas rises\n      persistently, raise it — otherwise small positions become uneconomical to liquidate and\n      quietly turn into bad debt.\n\n### Operating it\n\n- [ ] **Make sure liquidators actually exist before you take real deposits.** Every solvency\n      guarantee here assumes somebody is watching and has USDC ready. Run your own bot at\n      minimum; ideally have two or three independent parties running one. An unliquidated\n      market is an insolvent market.\n- [ ] **Monitor**: positions with `healthFactor` near `1e18`; oracle staleness (if `price()`\n      starts reverting, borrows and withdrawals halt while repayments keep working);\n      `BadDebtRecorded` events; utilisation against the caps.\n- [ ] **Verify the source** on Etherscan immediately after deploy (`forge verify-contract` or\n      `--verify` on the deploy script). Both contracts. Unverified contracts cannot be audited\n      by the people whose money is in them.\n\n### Token-specific things that will bite\n\n- **USDC is upgradeable, pausable, and has a blocklist.** If USDC pauses, repayments and\n  liquidations stop, and positions drift underwater with no remedy. If a *borrower* is\n  blocklisted they cannot repay and their position will eventually be liquidated through no\n  fault of their own. Neither is fixable in this contract; both are worth disclosing.\n- **Fee-on-transfer.** WETH and USDC do not charge fees today, but USDC's implementation can be\n  upgraded. Every inbound transfer is credited by measured balance delta rather than the\n  requested amount, and the debt token additionally requires the received amount to match\n  exactly, so a fee-charging USDC would revert loudly instead of silently under-crediting a\n  repayment.\n- **Donations.** Sending WETH or USDC directly to the market does not credit anyone. Donated\n  USDC becomes borrowable liquidity the owner can withdraw; donated WETH is stranded, since\n  `sweep` deliberately refuses to touch either market token.\n\n### Mainnet-specific\n\n- No L2 sequencer-uptime feed is needed here. **If this is ever redeployed to an L2, add one** —\n  without it, positions become liquidatable en masse the moment a sequencer comes back up after\n  an outage.\n- `SECONDS_PER_YEAR` is a flat 365 days. No leap-year handling; the rate is nominal, not exact.\n\n---\n\n## Known limitations\n\nThings a reviewer should know are deliberate, not oversights:\n\n1. **Single asset pair, single global rate.** No utilisation curve, no reserve factor. The task\n   called for a flat rate and this is one.\n2. **Owner-supplied liquidity only.** See above.\n3. **Bad debt is not socialised.** It sits on the position until the owner writes it off. With\n   one supplier that is the honest accounting; with public depositors it would need to be\n   distributed.\n4. **Liquidations are all-or-nothing below `Cv <= D * 1.05`.** Deliberate — see §2. It raises\n   the capital a liquidator needs for a deeply underwater position, but they receive all the\n   collateral in the same transaction, so it is flash-loanable.\n5. **No `permit` support.** Borrowers and liquidators need a separate `approve`.\n6. **Not upgradeable.** No proxy, no `delegatecall`, no upgrade authority — fixing anything\n   means a new deployment and a user migration. For a market this small, the removed attack\n   surface is worth more than the flexibility.\n\n## Running it\n\n```bash\nforge build\nforge test                       # 33 tests\nFOUNDRY_PROFILE=ci forge test    # 10,000 fuzz runs\n\nOWNER=0x... forge script script/Deploy.s.sol:Deploy \\\n  --rpc-url $MAINNET_RPC_URL --broadcast --verify --slow\n```\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2907,"cache_read_input_tokens":115712,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2907},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d85eed9d-2882-43d6-9ace-40e073b33c51","uuid":"55b61de4-a079-4553-a80a-a42e5af54cfb","timestamp":"2026-07-28T12:43:02.768Z","request_id":"req_011CdUUNaryJ5M2m8ui1n4oN"}

All the math uses `Math.mulDiv`, which carries the full\n512-bit intermediate, so the multiply-before-divide never overflows and never truncates early.\n\n### Debt\n\nDebt is stored as index-normalised **shares**, not as a balance:\n\n```\nborrowIndex starts at 1e27 (RAY) and only grows\non borrow:  shares += amount * RAY / borrowIndex      (rounded UP)\ndebt owed  = shares * borrowIndex / RAY                (rounded UP)\n```\n\n`accrueInterest()` moves the index forward whenever anything touches the market:\n\n```\nborrowIndex += borrowIndex * borrowRateBps * elapsedSeconds / (10_000 * 365 days)\n```\n\nThat is simple interest over each elapsed window, applied on top of the running index — so it\ncompounds across accruals, at whatever cadence the market is used. At 5% APR the difference\nfrom strict simple interest is small but not zero; it is not a rounding artifact, it is the\nmodel. Every rounding decision goes **against the borrower and in favour of the protocol**\n(shares round up when borrowing, down when repaying, debt rounds up when read), so rounding\ndust can never accumulate into a shortfall.\n\nIf nobody borrows for a year and then someone does, they are not charged for that year: the\naccrual clock advances even when `totalDebtShares == 0`.\n\n### Health factor\n\n```\nhealthFactor = collateralValue * liquidationThresholdBps / 10_000 * 1e18 / debt\n```\n\n- `healthFactor >= 1e18` → healthy.\n- `healthFactor < 1e18` → liquidatable.\n- No debt → `type(uint256).max`.\n\n**Two different ratios are used, and the distinction matters:**\n\n| Ratio | Value | Where it applies |\n|---|---|---|\n| Max LTV | 70% | `borrow`, `withdrawCollateral` — the *entry* gate |\n| Liquidation threshold | 85% | `liquidate` — the *exit* gate |\n\nA borrower can only take an action that leaves them at or under 70%. They become liquidatable\nat 85%. The 15-point gap is the buffer that stops a position from being openable and instantly\nliquidatable.\n\nWorked example — 10 WETH deposited, ETH at $2,000:\n\n```\ncollateralValue    = $20,000\nmax borrow (70%)   = $14,000\nliquidation at 85% = debt > $17,000, or equivalently ETH < debt / (10 * 0.85)\n```\n\nBorrow the full $14,000 and the position goes underwater at `14000 / 8.5 = $1,647.06` per ETH —\nan 18% drop.\n\nViews for integrators: `healthFactor`, `debtOf`, `collateralValueOf`, `maxBorrowable`,\n`maxWithdrawable`. All of them apply pending interest first, so they agree with what a\ntransaction in the same block would see.\n\n---\n\n## 2. What a liquidator has to do\n\n### The call\n\n```solidity\nmarket.liquidate(\n    address account,           // the borrower\n    uint256 repayAmount,       // ceiling on USDC you will pay; type(uint256).max = largest allowed\n    uint256 minCollateralOut,  // slippage guard in WETH — SET THIS\n    address to                 // where the seized WETH goes\n) returns (uint256 debtRepaid, uint256 collateralSeized);\n```\n\nPrerequisites: hold the USDC and `approve` the market for at least `debtRepaid`. You receive\nWETH (not ETH — unwrap yourself if you want ether).\n\n### Steps\n\n1. Watch positions. Off-chain, track `Borrowed` / `Repaid` / `CollateralDeposited` /\n   `CollateralWithdrawn` / `Liquidated` events to maintain the borrower set, and poll\n   `healthFactor(account)`. Anything under `1e18` is liquidatable.\n2. Call `previewLiquidation(account, repayAmount)` — a pure view returning the exact\n   `(debtRepaid, collateralSeized)` the real call would produce. Size your USDC approval from\n   `debtRepaid`, and your `minCollateralOut` from `collateralSeized`.\n3. Send the transaction with a real `minCollateralOut`. Do not pass `0`.\n\n### What you get\n\n```\ncollateralSeized = debtRepaid * (10_000 + 500) / 10_000 * priceScale / price\n```\n\nRepay $7,000 of USDC with ETH at $1,600 and you receive `7000 * 1.05 / 1600 = 4.59375 WETH`\n— $7,350 of collateral for $7,000, a $350 gross profit before gas.\n\n### How much you are allowed to repay\n\nLet `Cv` be collateral value and `D` be the debt.\n\n- **`Cv > D * 1.05`** — normal case. You may repay up to the **50% close factor**. `repayAmount`\n  is clamped down to it; anything larger just takes the maximum bite.\n- **`Cv <= D * 1.05`** — you must close out the **entire** position in one call. Partial\n  liquidation is refused here, and this is the one piece of behaviour most likely to surprise\n  you.\n\nThe reason for the second rule: a partial liquidation moves health from `Cv/D` to\n`(Cv - r(1+b))/(D - r)`, which is an improvement **only while `Cv > D * (1+b)`**. Below that\npoint each partial bite drives the position further underwater while leaving it liquidatable —\na still-solvent position gets ground down into bad debt by repeated small liquidations. So the\nmarket forces the whole thing to be closed at once instead.\n\nTwo consequences for you:\n\n- If your `repayAmount` is below what the close-out requires, the call reverts with\n  `FullCloseRequired(required)` and pulls nothing. `repayAmount` is a hard ceiling, never a\n  target the contract exceeds. Always `previewLiquidation` first.\n- In the band `D <= Cv < D * 1.05` your bonus is **less than 5%** — you pay the full debt and\n  receive all the collateral, so you earn `Cv - D`, which is somewhere between 0% and 5%. The\n  alternative would be to scale your repayment down to protect your headline bonus, which\n  would manufacture bad debt out of a solvent position. The market will not do that.\n\nThere is also a **dust rule**: if a partial liquidation would leave the borrower with less than\n`minDebt` (500 USDC) outstanding, the repayment is rounded up to the full debt, so no position\nis ever left too small to be worth the gas to liquidate.\n\n### Bad debt\n\nIf the collateral cannot cover the debt even at a 0% bonus, you seize all of it, repay\n`Cv / 1.05`, and the remainder is a loss to the supply side. The market emits\n`BadDebtRecorded`. It does **not** socialise the loss across other borrowers; the owner clears\nthe accounting entry with `writeOffBadDebt(account)`.\n\n### MEV\n\nLiquidations are competitive and public. Assume every profitable one is contested:\n\n- `minCollateralOut` is your only on-chain protection against the price moving between\n  broadcast and inclusion. Set it from `previewLiquidation` with a tolerance you have chosen.\n- Send through a private mempool (Flashbots Protect, MEV-Blocker) — a public liquidation\n  transaction is trivially frontrun by a searcher who copies it.\n- Back-run the Chainlink feed update rather than polling on a timer: `ChainlinkPriceOracle`\n  reads `latestRoundData()`, so a position becomes liquidatable in the same block the feed\n  updates. That transaction is the signal.\n\n---\n\n## 3. What the operator has to get right on mainnet\n\n### Before the first deploy\n\n- [ ] **Verify the three addresses in `script/Deploy.s.sol` against Etherscan.** WETH\n      `0xC02aaA...756Cc2`, USDC `0xA0b869...06eB48`, ETH/USD feed `0x5f4eC3...5b8419`. A wrong\n      feed address is a silent total loss, not a revert.\n- [ ] **Point the oracle at the Chainlink *proxy*, not the underlying aggregator.** The\n      aggregator gets rotated; the proxy is the stable address.\n- [ ] **Set `maxStaleness` from the feed's real heartbeat.** Mainnet ETH/USD is 3600s, so the\n      script uses 3900s. Too tight and the market halts on a slow round; too loose and you\n      price positions off yesterday's number.\n- [ ] **Sanity-check `minPrice`/`maxPrice`.** The script uses $100–$100,000. These exist to\n      reject a broken or migrated feed. Note the failure mode they *cannot* fully fix: the\n      underlying Chainlink aggregator has its own `minAnswer`/`maxAnswer` circuit breakers, and\n      during a genuine crash below `minAnswer` the feed keeps reporting the floor. That is the\n      Venus/BNB failure mode. If ETH ever traded below your `minPrice`, this market would value\n      collateral too high and under-liquidate.\n- [ ] **Run `slither .` and `forge test --fuzz-runs 10000` and resolve everything.** Neither\n      slither nor mythril is installed in this workspace, so **the static-analysis pass has not\n      been run** — it is still outstanding and must happen before deploy. The test suite\n      (33 tests, fuzz included) does pass at 10,000 runs.\n- [ ] **Get an audit.** This is unaudited code holding user collateral.\n\n### Ownership\n\n- [ ] **`OWNER` must be a multisig or timelock, never an EOA.** The deploy script enforces\n      `owner.code.length > 0` as a blunt guard against fat-fingering an EOA, but that only\n      proves it is a contract, not that it is a *good* contract.\n- [ ] Ownership uses `Ownable2Step` — the new owner must accept. Don't leave a pending\n      transfer half-finished.\n- [ ] Put `setBorrowRateBps` behind a timelock. It is bounded at 50% APR in code, but a rate\n      change is still an economic action users deserve notice of.\n\n### What the owner can and cannot do\n\nWorth being explicit with users, because it defines the trust model:\n\n| Can | Cannot |\n|---|---|\n| Set the borrow rate (capped at 50% APR) | Change the oracle — it is `immutable` |\n| Set borrow / collateral caps and `minDebt` | Change LTV, threshold, bonus, or close factor — all `immutable` |\n| Pause new deposits and new borrows | Block `repay`, `withdrawCollateral`, or `liquidate` |\n| Withdraw idle USDC (`withdrawSupply`) | Touch WETH collateral — `sweep` rejects both market tokens |\n| Write off bad debt on a zero-collateral position | Seize or move a healthy borrower's position |\n\nTwo deliberate choices behind that table:\n\n**Pause is not a freeze.** `pause()` gates `depositCollateral` and `borrow` only. Repay,\nwithdraw, and liquidate stay open. A pause that traps a healthy borrower's collateral is a\ncensorship vector and a liquidation-cascade risk, so this one cannot. Even so, a single key\nthat can halt new borrowing is real power — it belongs behind the same multisig, and users\nshould be told it exists.\n\n**The oracle cannot be swapped.** Migrating to a new feed means deploying a new market and\nletting users migrate. That is inconvenient by design: an oracle address that a hot key can\nchange is a total-loss switch.\n\n### Liquidity\n\nThis market has **no public supplier side**. The owner supplies USDC with `supply()` and\nwithdraws it with `withdrawSupply()`; interest accrues to that same pool. This keeps the\ncontract small and sidesteps lender-share accounting and the ERC-4626 inflation attack\nentirely — but it means:\n\n- The owner can withdraw all idle USDC at any time. That cannot touch collateral or claw back\n  an outstanding loan, but it can leave the market unable to serve new borrows. It is a real\n  trust assumption; state it publicly.\n- Adding public depositors later is **not** a parameter change. It needs a share token with a\n  virtual-offset mitigation, a reserve factor, and a utilisation-aware rate model. That is a\n  different contract, not a config flag.\n\n### Caps and sizing\n\n- [ ] Start `borrowCap` and `collateralCap` small (the script defaults to 2M USDC / 1000 WETH)\n      and raise them as the market proves out. Lowering a cap only blocks new borrows; it never\n      makes an existing position liquidatable.\n- [ ] `minDebt` (500 USDC) must stay above the gas cost of a liquidation. If mainnet gas rises\n      persistently, raise it — otherwise small positions become uneconomical to liquidate and\n      quietly turn into bad debt.\n\n### Operating it\n\n- [ ] **Make sure liquidators actually exist before you take real deposits.** Every solvency\n      guarantee here assumes somebody is watching and has USDC ready. Run your own bot at\n      minimum; ideally have two or three independent parties running one. An unliquidated\n      market is an insolvent market.\n- [ ] **Monitor**: positions with `healthFactor` near `1e18`; oracle staleness (if `price()`\n      starts reverting, borrows and withdrawals halt while repayments keep working);\n      `BadDebtRecorded` events; utilisation against the caps.\n- [ ] **Verify the source** on Etherscan immediately after deploy (`forge verify-contract` or\n      `--verify` on the deploy script). Both contracts. Unverified contracts cannot be audited\n      by the people whose money is in them.\n\n### Token-specific things that will bite\n\n- **USDC is upgradeable, pausable, and has a blocklist.** If USDC pauses, repayments and\n  liquidations stop, and positions drift underwater with no remedy. If a *borrower* is\n  blocklisted they cannot repay and their position will eventually be liquidated through no\n  fault of their own. Neither is fixable in this contract; both are worth disclosing.\n- **Fee-on-transfer.** WETH and USDC do not charge fees today, but USDC's implementation can be\n  upgraded. Every inbound transfer is credited by measured balance delta rather than the\n  requested amount, and the debt token additionally requires the received amount to match\n  exactly, so a fee-charging USDC would revert loudly instead of silently under-crediting a\n  repayment.\n- **Donations.** Sending WETH or USDC directly to the market does not credit anyone. Donated\n  USDC becomes borrowable liquidity the owner can withdraw; donated WETH is stranded, since\n  `sweep` deliberately refuses to touch either market token.\n\n### Mainnet-specific\n\n- No L2 sequencer-uptime feed is needed here. **If this is ever redeployed to an L2, add one** —\n  without it, positions become liquidatable en masse the moment a sequencer comes back up after\n  an outage.\n- `SECONDS_PER_YEAR` is a flat 365 days. No leap-year handling; the rate is nominal, not exact.\n\n---\n\n## Known limitations\n\nThings a reviewer should know are deliberate, not oversights:\n\n1. **Single asset pair, single global rate.** No utilisation curve, no reserve factor. The task\n   called for a flat rate and this is one.\n2. **Owner-supplied liquidity only.** See above.\n3. **Bad debt is not socialised.** It sits on the position until the owner writes it off. With\n   one supplier that is the honest accounting; with public depositors it would need to be\n   distributed.\n4. **Liquidations are all-or-nothing below `Cv <= D * 1.05`.** Deliberate — see §2. It raises\n   the capital a liquidator needs for a deeply underwater position, but they receive all the\n   collateral in the same transaction, so it is flash-loanable.\n5. **No `permit` support.** Borrowers and liquidators need a separate `approve`.\n6. **Not upgradeable.** No proxy, no `delegatecall`, no upgrade authority — fixing anything\n   means a new deployment and a user migration. For a market this small, the removed attack\n   surface is worth more than the flexibility.\n\n## Running it\n\n```bash\nforge build\nforge test                       # 33 tests\nFOUNDRY_PROFILE=ci forge test    # 10,000 fuzz runs\n\nOWNER=0x... forge script script/Deploy.s.sol:Deploy \\\n  --rpc-url $MAINNET_RPC_URL --broadcast --verify --slow\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdUUWNS5v6XLFPxWWHhxq","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. `forge build` passes clean, `forge test` is 33/33 including fuzz at 10,000 runs.\n\n## What's there\n\n- **`src/LendingMarket.sol`** — the market. WETH collateral, USDC debt, 70% LTV / 85% liquidation threshold / 5% bonus / 50% close factor / 5% flat APR, all `immutable` except the rate and caps.\n- **`src/ChainlinkPriceOracle.sol`** — immutable wrapper on the ETH/USD feed. No owner, no setters; staleness, round-completeness, and min/max sanity checks, and it reverts rather than returning a doubtful price.\n- **`script/Deploy.s.sol`** — mainnet addresses and parameters, refuses an EOA owner.\n- **`NOTES.md`** — health math, the liquidator's playbook, the operator checklist.\n\n## Two things worth your attention\n\n**The liquidation close-factor rule changed during implementation, and it's a real finding.** A partial liquidation only improves a position's health while collateral value `> debt × (1 + bonus)`. Below that, each capped 50% bite pushes the position *further* underwater while leaving it liquidatable — I confirmed by fuzzing that repeated liquidations grind a still-solvent position down into bad debt. So below that cutoff the market now forces the whole position to be closed in one call. Merely *permitting* a full close wasn't enough: a liquidator could still take a small degrading bite, which the fuzzer also found.\n\nA consequence: in the band `debt ≤ Cv < debt × 1.05` the liquidator earns *less* than 5% (they pay the full debt, take all collateral, earn `Cv − debt`). Scaling their repayment down to protect the headline bonus would manufacture bad debt out of a solvent position, so the contract doesn't. `repayAmount` is a hard ceiling — if a close-out needs more, it reverts with `FullCloseRequired(required)` rather than pulling more than authorized.\n\n**One checklist item is outstanding.** Slither and mythril aren't installed here, so the static-analysis pass has not been run. I flagged it in NOTES.md as required-before-deploy rather than installing tooling unasked. The code is also unaudited.\n\nDesign decisions you may want to revisit: USDC liquidity is **owner-supplied** — there's no public depositor side, which keeps lender-share accounting and the ERC-4626 inflation attack out of scope entirely, but means the owner can withdraw all idle USDC (never collateral). And the oracle is `immutable` — swapping feeds means a new deployment. Both are argued in NOTES.md under Known Limitations; say the word if you'd rather have either one."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":258,"cache_read_input_tokens":126298,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":258},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d85eed9d-2882-43d6-9ace-40e073b33c51","uuid":"f177710d-79c0-40c3-9478-d67ee67ea708","timestamp":"2026-07-28T12:43:27.015Z","request_id":"req_011CdUUWLzmDnGfN5sPrPndP"}
